\kappa_{x,\alpha}=\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}\right)
. Here,
\alpha
is seen as a multiset of
[n]\times[p]
, i.e
\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}
contains
\alpha_{ij}
copies of
X_{ij}
.
The sparse clustering model is a special case of the latent model (
1
), with

Z=(k^{*},z,\varepsilon),\quad\delta_{ij}(Z)=z_{j}\varepsilon_{i},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\theta_{i,j}(Z)=(k^{*}_{i},j)\kern 5.0pt.

Combining Proposition
2.1
and Theorem
2.5
, we need to upper bound, for any decomposition
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}=\alpha
, with
|\beta_{s}|=2
, the cumulant

C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}=\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\prod_{(ij)\in\beta_{1}}\varepsilon_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\beta_{1}}(k^{*})},\ldots,\prod_{(ij)\in\beta_{1}}\varepsilon_{i}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\beta_{l}}(k^{*})}\right),

with

\Omega_{\beta}(k^{*}):=\left\{\big|\left\{(k^{*}_{i},j):\kern 5.0pt(i,j)\in\beta\right\}\big|=1\right\}\cap\left\{\forall j\in col(\beta),\kern 5.0ptz_{j}=1\right\}
.

Building on the recursive Bound (
18
), we derive in Section
C.1
the following upper-bound.

Lemma D.1
.

We recall that
\#\alpha
stands for the number of indices
i\in[1,n]
such that
\alpha_{i:}\neq 0
, that
r_{\alpha}
stands for the number of indices
j\in[1,p]
such that
\alpha_{:j}\neq 0
and that
|\alpha|:=\sum_{ij}\alpha_{ij}
.
We have

C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}\leq\rho^{r_{\alpha}}|\alpha|^{|\alpha|-r_{\alpha}-\#\alpha+2}\min\left(\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-1},\frac{1}{K}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Combining this bound with (
55
) and counting the number of partitions
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\alpha)
for which
C_{x,\beta_{1}(\pi),\ldots,\beta_{l}(\pi)}\neq 0
, we prove in Section
D.7
the following upper-bound on
|\kappa_{x,\alpha}|
.

Lemma D.2
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}
non-zero. We have

|\kappa_{x,\alpha}|\leq\lambda^{|\alpha|}\rho^{r_{\alpha}}|\alpha|^{2\left(|\alpha|-r_{\alpha}-\#\alpha+2\right)}\min\left(\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-1},\frac{1}{K}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

The last stage is to prune the multisets
\alpha
for which
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.
The next lemma gives necessary conditions for having
\kappa_{x,\alpha}\neq 0
. For this purpose, it is convenient, as in the proof of Theorem
3.1
, to introduce a bipartite multigraph
\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}
on two disjoint sets
U=\left\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\right\}
and
V=\left\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{p}\right\}
with
\alpha_{ij}
edges between
u_{i}
and
v_{j}
, for any
i,j\in[n]\times[p]
. We write
\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{-}
the restriction of
\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}
to non-isolated nodes. We denote
U(\alpha)
the elements of
U
which are nodes of
\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{-}
and
V(\alpha)
the elements of
V
which are nodes of
\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{-}
. We refer to Section
D.8
for a proof of this lemma.

Lemma D.3
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}
be non-zero. If
\kappa_{x,\alpha}\neq 0
, then

•

u_{1},u_{2}\in U(\alpha)
;

•

\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\alpha}\cup\left\{(u_{1},u_{2})\right\}
is connected;

•

All the elements of
U(\alpha)
and
V(\alpha)
are of degree at least 2.

In particular, we have
\#\alpha\geq 2
,
|\alpha|\geq 2r_{\alpha}
and
|\alpha|\geq 2\#\alpha
.

Remark 9
.

In fact, we can prove that
\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\alpha}
is connected (see
[
28
]
), but it is sufficient and more straightforward using Theorem
2.5
to prove that
\mathcal{G}^{-}_{\alpha}\cup\left\{(u_{1},u_{2})\right\}
is connected.

Let
d\in[2,D]
,
m,r
such that
d\geq 2\max\left(r,m\right)
. Since the conditions of Lemma
D.3
are more restrictive than the ones of Lemma
C.3
, we can apply Lemma
C.4
. Thus, there exists at most
n^{m-2}p^{r}d^{3(d-r-m+2)}
matrices
\alpha
satisfying the conditions of Lemma
D.3
with
|\alpha|=d
,
\#\alpha=m
and
r_{\alpha}=r
.

Then, using Proposition
2.1
together with Lemma
D.2
, and pruning the terms that do not satisfy the conditions of Lemma
D.3
, we get

\displaystyle corr_{\leq D}^{2}-\frac{1}{K^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\neq 0,\kern 4.09723pt|\alpha|\leq D}\kappa_{\alpha}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{d\in[D]}\underset{2\leq m\leq d/2}{\sum_{r\leq d/2}}\left(d^{7}\lambda^{2}\right)^{d}\left(\frac{n}{d^{7}}\right)^{m-2}\left(\frac{\rho^{2}p}{d^{7}}\right)^{r}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K^{2}}\right)^{m+r-\frac{d}{2}-2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Let us fix
r\geq 1
,
m\geq 2
and
d\geq\max\left(2r,2m\right)
and let us upper-bound the quantity

A_{d,r,m}:=\left(d^{7}\lambda^{2}\right)^{d}\left(\frac{n}{d^{7}}\right)^{m-2}\left(\frac{\rho^{2}p}{d^{7}}\right)^{r}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K^{2}}\right)^{m+r-\frac{d}{2}-2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

First, let us suppose that
d<2(m-2)+2r
.
Decomposing into sums of positive terms
m-2=m-2-(d/2-r)+(d/2-r)
and
d=2r+2(d/2-r)
, we get

\displaystyle A_{d,r,m}=

\displaystyle\left(d^{7}\lambda^{2}\right)^{d}\left(\frac{n}{d^{7}}\right)^{m-2}\left(\frac{\rho^{2}p}{d^{7}}\right)^{r}\left(\frac{1}{K^{2}}\right)^{m+r-\frac{d}{2}-2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D^{7(d-m-r+2)}\lambda^{2d}n^{m-2}\left(\rho^{2}p\right)^{r}\left(\frac{1}{K^{2}}\right)^{m+r-\frac{d}{2}-2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D^{7(d-m-r+2)}\left(\lambda^{4}\rho^{2}p\right)^{r}\left(\lambda^{4}n\right)^{d/2-r}\left(\frac{n}{K^{2}}\right)^{m-2-(d/2-r)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D^{7(d-m-r+2)}\left(\lambda^{4}\rho^{2}p\right)^{r-(m-2-(d/2-r))}\left(\frac{\lambda^{4}\rho^{2}pn}{K^{2}}\right)^{m-2-(d/2-r)}\left(\lambda^{4}n\right)^{d/2-r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{D^{7}\bar{\Delta}^{4}}{p}\right)^{r-(m-2-(d/2-r))}\left(\frac{\bar{\Delta}^{4}n}{pK^{2}}\right)^{m-2-(d/2-r)}\left(\frac{D^{7}\bar{\Delta}^{4}n}{p^{2}\rho^{2}}\right)^{d/2-r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\zeta^{r-(m-2-(d/2-r))}\zeta^{m-2-(d/2-r)}\zeta^{d/2-r}=\zeta^{\frac{d}{2}}\kern 5.0pt,

where we used in the fifth line that
\lambda^{2}=\bar{\Delta}^{2}/(p\rho)
and the definition (
67
) of
\zeta
.

On the other hand, if
d\geq 2r+2(m-2)
, we decompose
d=d-\left(2(m-2)+2r\right)+2(m-2)+2r
to get

\displaystyle A_{d,r,m}=

\displaystyle\left(d^{7}\lambda^{2}\right)^{d}\left(\frac{n}{d^{7}}\right)^{m-2}\left(\frac{\rho^{2}p}{d^{7}}\right)^{r}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K^{2}}\right)^{m+r-\frac{d}{2}-2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(D^{7}\lambda^{2}\right)^{d}\left(\frac{n}{D^{7}}\right)^{m-2}\left(\frac{\rho^{2}p}{D^{7}}\right)^{r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(D^{7}\lambda^{2}\right)^{d-2r-2(m-2)}\left(D^{7}\lambda^{4}n\right)^{m-2}\left(D^{7}\lambda^{4}\rho^{2}p\right)^{r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(D^{7}\frac{\bar{\Delta}^{2}}{p\rho}\right)^{d-2r-2(m-2)}\left(D^{7}\frac{\bar{\Delta}^{4}}{p^{2}\rho^{2}}n\right)^{m-2}\left(D^{7}\frac{\bar{\Delta}^{4}}{p}\right)^{r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\zeta}^{d-2r-2(m-2)}\zeta^{m-2}\zeta^{r}=\zeta^{\frac{d}{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

We can conclude the proof of the theorem with

\displaystyle corr_{\leq D}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\left(1+\sum_{d\in[2,D]}\underset{2\leq m\leq d/2}{\sum_{r\leq d/2}}\zeta^{\frac{d}{2}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{K^{2}}\left(1+\sum_{d\in[2,D]}\frac{d(d-1)}{2}\zeta^{\frac{d}{2}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{K^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\zeta}{\left(1-\sqrt{\zeta}\right)^{3}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

Let us fix a decomposition
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}=\alpha
, with
|\beta_{s}|=2
and let us upper-bound
|C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}|
. For having
C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}
, it is necessary that each
\beta_{s}
is supported on only one column. Thus, we can write
\beta_{s}=\left\{(i_{s},j_{s});(i^{\prime}_{s},j_{s})\right\}
. Henceforth, we use the convention
i_{0}=1
,
i^{\prime}_{0}=2
, and
j_{0}=0
. For
S\subseteq[l]
, we write
\beta[S]=\left\{\beta_{s},s\in S\right\}
. Building on (
18
), we get, for all
S\subseteq[l]
, the recursion formula

\displaystyle|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in\left\{0\right\}\cup S,\kern 5.0ptk^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right]\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in S,\kern 5.0ptz_{j_{s}}=1\right]+

\displaystyle+\sum_{S^{\prime}\subseteq S}|C_{x,\beta[S^{\prime}]}|\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in\left\{0\right\}\cup S^{\prime},\kern 5.0ptk^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right]\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in S,\kern 5.0ptz_{j_{s}}=1\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S}}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in\left\{0\right\}\cup S,\kern 5.0ptk^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right]+\sum_{S^{\prime}\subseteq S}|C_{x,\beta[S^{\prime}]}|\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in S\setminus S^{\prime},\kern 5.0ptk^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right]\kern 5.0pt,

where, for
R\subseteq[l]
,
\alpha_{R}=\sum_{s\in R}\beta_{R}
.

Let us compute, for any subset
R\subseteq[0,l]
, the quantity
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in R,\kern 5.0ptk^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i_{s}}\right]
. To do so, let us define, as in Section
C.1
,
\mathcal{V}
the graph on
[0,l]
defined by: for
s,s^{\prime}\geq 0
, there is an edge between
s
and
s^{\prime}
if and only if
\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}\cap\left\{i_{s^{\prime}},i^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}\right\}\neq\emptyset
. For
R\subseteq[0,l]
, we write
\mathcal{V}[R]
the restriction of
\mathcal{V}
to
R
and
cc(\mathcal{V}[R])
the number of connected components of this graph. As in Section
C.1
, we obtain, when
0\in R
,
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in R,\kern 5.0ptk^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right]=\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{|supp(\alpha_{R\setminus\left\{0\right\}})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[R]\right)}
, and, when
0\notin R
,
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in R,\kern 5.0ptk^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right]=\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{R}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[R]\right)}
. In turn, for all
S\subseteq[l]
, we have

|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S}}}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{|supp(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\{0\}])}+\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}|C_{x,\beta[S^{\prime}]}|\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}])}\kern 5.0pt.

(68)

The next lemma whose proof is postponed to Section
D.2
, prunes subsets
S\subseteq[l]
such that
C_{x,\beta[S]}=0
. To do so, we introduce the graph
W
on
[0,l]
with an edge between
s,s^{\prime}\in[0,l]
if and only if
j_{s}=j_{s^{\prime}}
or
\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}\cap\left\{i_{s^{\prime}},i^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}\right\}\neq\emptyset
(we recall that for
s=0
, we write
j_{0}=0
). For
S\subseteq[l]
and
j\in\cup_{s\in S\cup\left\{0\right\}}\left\{j_{s}\right\}=col(\alpha_{s})\cup\left\{0\right\}
, we write
S_{j}=\left\{s\in S,j_{s}=j\right\}
(in particular
S_{0}=\left\{0\right\}
).
In the following, we denote
\mathcal{S}([l])
the collection of subset
S
of
[l]
satisfying;

1.

\mathcal{W}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
is connected;

2.

If
S\neq\emptyset
, then for all
j\in col(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{0\right\}
, there exist
\underline{i}\neq\underline{i}^{\prime}\in\cup_{s\in S_{j}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}
such that both
\underline{i}
and
\underline{i^{\prime}}
are in
\cup_{s\in S\setminus S_{j}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}
;

3.

For all
i\in supp(\alpha_{S})\setminus\left\{1,2\right\}
,
|\left(\alpha_{S}\right)_{i:}|\geq 2
.

In particular, the second property implies that, as long as
S\neq\emptyset
, we have

\{1,2\}\subset supp(\alpha_{S})
.

Lemma D.4
.

For
S\subseteq[l]
, if
C_{x,\beta[S]}\neq 0
, then
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
.

Pruning the other terms in (
68
) leads to, for all
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
,

|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S}}}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{|supp(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\{0\}])}+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}|C_{x,\beta[S^{\prime}]}|\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

(69)

In the following, let us define recursively a function
f
on
\mathcal{S}([l])
satisfying, for all
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
,

f(S)=1+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}f(S^{\prime})\kern 5.0pt.

(70)

In particular,
f(\emptyset)=1
. The next lemma, proved in Section
D.3
, relies on the connectivity of
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
for
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
, to bound
|C_{x,\beta[S]}|
with respect to this function
f
.

Lemma D.5
.

For all
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
, we have
|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S}}}\min\left(\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}-1},\frac{1}{K}\right)f(S)
.

It remains to upper-bound
f(S)
for all
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
.

Lemma D.6
.

For all non empty
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
, we have
f(S)\leq|\alpha|^{|\alpha_{S}|-\#\alpha_{S}-r_{\alpha_{S}}+2}
.

Applying Lemma
D.6
and Lemma
D.5
to
S=[l]
leads to

C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}\leq\rho^{r_{\alpha}}|\alpha|^{|\alpha|-r_{\alpha}-\#\alpha+2}\min\left(\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-1},\frac{1}{K}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

which concludes the proof of the lemma.

D.2
Proof of Lemma
D.4

Let
S\subseteq[l]
. Let us suppose that
S\notin\mathcal{S}([l])
and let us prove that
C_{x,\beta[S]}=0
. The set
\mathcal{S}([l])
is an intersection of three constraints. We shall suppose that one of these constraints is not satisfied;

1.

Let us first suppose that
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
is not connected. Let
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
a partition of
S\cup\left\{0\right\}
with no edges of
\mathcal{W}
connecting them. We suppose by symmetry that
0\in C_{1}
. Then, the family of random variables
((\varepsilon_{i},k^{*}_{i})_{i\in\cup_{s\in C_{1}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}},\left(z_{j}\right)_{j\in\cup_{s\in C_{1}\setminus\left\{0\right\}}\{j_{s}\}})
is independent of the family
((\varepsilon_{i},k^{*}_{i})_{i\in\cup_{s\in C_{2}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}},\left(z_{j}\right)_{j\in\cup_{s\in C_{2}}\{j_{s}\}})
. Then, Lemma
B.2
implies that
C_{x,\beta[S]}=0
.

2.

Let us now suppose that there exists
j_{0}\in col(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{0\right\}
with at most one element in
\cup_{s\in S_{j_{0}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}
which is also in
\cup_{s\in S\setminus S_{j_{0}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}
. Let us denote
\underline{i}
this element.
Then,
(\epsilon_{\underline{i}})
is independent of

(\epsilon_{i_{s}}\epsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}})_{s\in S\setminus S_{j_{0}}}
. Indeed, since the
\epsilon_{i}
’s are distributed as independent rademacher, the conditional distribution of
(\epsilon_{i_{s}}\epsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}})_{s\in S\setminus S_{j_{0}}}
does not depend on the value
(\epsilon_{\underline{i}})
. Since, apart from
\epsilon_{\underline{i}}
, all the other
\epsilon_{i}
with
i\in\cup_{s\in S_{j_{0}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}
do not occur in
(\epsilon_{i_{s}}\epsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}})_{s\in S\setminus S_{j_{0}}}
, we deduce that
(\epsilon_{i})_{i\in\cup_{s\in S_{j_{0}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}}
is independent of
(\epsilon_{i_{s}}\epsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}})_{s\in S\setminus S_{j_{0}}}
. We have proved that

(\varepsilon_{i_{s}}\varepsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}})_{s\in S_{j_{0}}}
is independent of
(\varepsilon_{i_{s}}\varepsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}})_{s\in S\setminus S_{j_{0}}}
. Arguing similarly, we get that
(z_{j_{0}},(\varepsilon_{i_{s}}\varepsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\mathbf{1}\{k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\})_{s\in S_{j_{0}}})
is independent of
((z_{j})_{j\neq j_{0}},(\varepsilon_{i_{s}}\varepsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\mathbf{1}\{k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\})_{s\in S\setminus S_{j_{0}}})
. From Lemma
B.2
, we conclude that
C_{x,\beta[S]}=0
.

3.

Let us finally suppose that there exists
\underline{i}\in supp(\alpha_{S})\setminus\left\{1,2\right\}
with
|\left(\alpha_{S}\right)_{\underline{i}:}|=1
. Let
s_{0}
the unique element of
S
such that
\underline{i}\in supp(\beta_{s_{0}})
; we suppose
\underline{i}=i_{s_{0}}
for exemple. The random variable
\varepsilon_{\underline{i}}
is symmetric and independent from all the other random variables. Hence, changing
\varepsilon_{\underline{i}}
to
-\varepsilon_{\underline{i}}
does not change the joint law of all the random variables and thus, by multilinearity of the cumulant, we have

\displaystyle C_{x,\beta[S]}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\left(\varepsilon_{i_{s}}\varepsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\mathbf{1}_{z_{j_{s}}\neq 0}\mathbf{1}_{k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}}\right)_{s\in S}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\left(\varepsilon_{i_{s}}\varepsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\mathbf{1}_{z_{j_{s}}\neq 0}\mathbf{1}_{k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}}\right)_{s\in S\setminus\{s_{0}\}},-\varepsilon_{i_{s_{0}}}\varepsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s_{0}}}\mathbf{1}_{z_{j_{s_{0}}}\neq 0}\mathbf{1}_{k^{*}_{i_{s_{0}}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s_{0}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\left(\varepsilon_{i_{s}}\varepsilon_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\mathbf{1}_{z_{j_{s}}\neq 0}\mathbf{1}_{k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}}\right)_{s\in S}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-C_{x,\beta[S]}\kern 5.0pt,

which, in turn, implies that
C_{x,\beta[S]}=0
.

D.3
Proof of Lemma
D.5

Let us prove by induction that, for all
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
,

|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S}}}\min(\frac{1}{K},\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}-1})f(S)\ .

The initialization is trivial since
C_{x,\beta[\emptyset]}=\mathrm{Cum}(x)=\frac{1}{K}
and
|\alpha_{\emptyset}|=0
.

Induction

Let
S\neq\emptyset\in\mathcal{S}([l])
and let us suppose that the result holds for all
S^{\prime}\subsetneq S
with
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])
. Since
S\neq\emptyset
,
we know from the remark below the definition of
\mathcal{S}([l])
that
\{1,2\}\subset supp(\alpha_{S})
.

Applying Inequality (
69
) to
S
together with the induction hypothesis leads to

\displaystyle|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq

\displaystyle\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S}}}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\{0\}])}+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}|C_{x,\beta[S^{\prime}]}|\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S}}}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\{0\}])}

\displaystyle+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}f(S^{\prime})\min\left(\frac{1}{K},\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S^{\prime}}|}{2}-1}\right)\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)}\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}+r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

Let us remark that
r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}+r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}\geq r_{\alpha_{S}}
.
Since
\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\{0\}])\geq 1
and since
\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)\geq 0
, we directly deduce that
|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq\frac{1}{K}f(S)\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S}}}
.

It remains to prove that
|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq\rho^{r_{\alpha_{S}}}f(S)\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}-1}
. Let us isolate the term
S^{\prime}=\emptyset
in the sum.

\displaystyle\rho^{-r_{\alpha_{S}}}|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\{0\}])}+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{\emptyset\neq S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}f(S^{\prime})\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S^{\prime}}|}{2}-1+\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{K}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S]\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

The next lemma uses the connectivity of the graph
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
in order to lower bound the exposants of the above inequality. We refer to Section
D.4
for its proof.

Lemma D.7
.

For any subset
R\subseteq[0,l]
,
cc\left(\mathcal{W}[R]\right)-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[R]\right)\geq r_{\alpha_{R}}-\frac{|\alpha_{R}|}{2}
.

Applying Lemma
D.7
together with the fact that
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
is connected leads us to

\displaystyle\frac{|C_{x,\beta[S]}|}{\rho^{\alpha_{R}}}\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}-1}

\displaystyle+\underset{\emptyset\neq S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}f(S^{\prime})\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S^{\prime}}|}{2}-1+\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}|}{2}-cc\left(\mathcal{W}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{1+\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}-cc\left(\mathcal{W}[S]\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

The next lemma uses again the connectivity of
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
in order to lower-bound the exposants in the sum above. We refer to Section
D.5
for its proof.

Lemma D.8
.

For any subset
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
that both belonf to
\mathcal{S}([l])
and such that
S^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
, we have

\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}+\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}-cc\left(\mathcal{W}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)\geq\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}\kern 5.0pt.

For the term
S^{\prime}=\emptyset
, we use the fact that
cc\left(\mathcal{W}[S]\right)\leq 2
to get the desired inequality

\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{1+\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}-cc\left(\mathcal{W}[S]\right)}\leq\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}-\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}-1}\kern 5.0pt.

We deduce from this and Lemma
D.8
that

\displaystyle\frac{|C_{x,\beta[S]}|}{\rho^{\alpha_{R}}}\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-1}\left(1+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}f(S^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle f(S)\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-1}\kern 5.0pt,

which concludes the induction and the proof of the lemma.

D.4
Proof of Lemma
D.7

Let us fix
R\subseteq[0,l]
. We need to prove that

|\alpha_{R}|\geq 2\left(r_{\alpha_{R}}-cc\left(\mathcal{W}[R]\right)+cc\left(\mathcal{V}[R]\right)\right)\kern 5.0pt.

By definition,
\mathcal{V}(R)
is a subgraph of
\mathcal{W}_{r}
. Hence,
to show this inequality, it is sufficient to prove it for all connected components of
\mathcal{W}[R]
. We can therefore suppose without loss of generality that
\mathcal{W}[R]
is connected.

Denote
q=cc(\mathcal{V}[R])
and let us write
cc_{1},\ldots,cc_{q}
the collection of the connected components of
\mathcal{V}[R]
. Since the graph
\mathcal{W}[R]
is connected, we can, up to a reordering of the
cc_{l}
’s, suppose that, for all
q^{\prime}\in[2,q]
,
\mathcal{W}[R]
has an edge connecting
cc_{q^{\prime}}
to
\left(\cup_{q^{\prime\prime}<q^{\prime}}cc_{q^{\prime\prime}}\right)
. In the following, for all
q^{\prime}\in[q]
, we write
\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}}=\sum_{s\in cc_{q^{\prime}}}\beta_{s}
and
\alpha^{(q^{\prime})}=\sum_{q^{\prime\prime}\leq q^{\prime}}\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime\prime}}}
. Let us prove by induction over
q^{\prime}\in[q]
that

|\alpha^{(q^{\prime})}|\geq 2r_{\alpha^{(q^{\prime})}}+2(q^{\prime}-1)\kern 5.0pt.

Initialization

For all
j\in col(\alpha^{(1)})
, there exists
s\in cc_{1}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
such that
j_{s}=j
(we recall that
\beta_{s}=\left\{(i_{s},j_{s});(i^{\prime}_{s},j_{s})\right\}
). Thus,
|\left(\alpha^{(1)}\right)_{:j}|\geq|\beta_{s}|=2
. We deduce that
|\alpha^{(1)}|\geq 2r_{\alpha^{(1)}}
.

Induction

Let us suppose that the result holds for some
q^{\prime}\in[q-1]
and let us prove that it still holds for
q^{\prime}+1
. As for the initalisation, we have
|\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}+1}}|\geq 2r_{\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}+1}}}
. Since
\mathcal{W}

has an edge connecting
cc_{q^{\prime}+1}
to
\left(\cup_{q^{\prime\prime}\leq q^{\prime}}cc_{q^{\prime\prime}}\right)
whereas
\mathcal{V}
does not have any, we know that
col(\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}+1}})
intersects
col(\alpha^{(q^{\prime})})
. Together with the induction hypothesis, this implies that

|\alpha^{(q^{\prime}+1)}|\geq 2r_{\alpha^{(q^{\prime})}}+2(q^{\prime}-1)+2r_{\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}+1}}}\geq 2r_{\alpha^{(q^{\prime}+1)}}+2q^{\prime}\kern 5.0pt,

and concludes the induction.

D.5
Proof of Lemma
D.8

Let
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
be a non-empty set. Since
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])
is non-empty, we know that
\left\{1,2\right\}\subset supp(\alpha_{S^{\prime}})
. Let us prove the inequality

\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}+\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}\geq\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}+cc\left(\mathcal{W}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Let
q=cc\left(\mathcal{W}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)
and let us write
cc_{1},\ldots,cc_{q}
the collection of the connected components of
\mathcal{W}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]
. Since the graph
\mathcal{W}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]
does not have any edge between the
cc_{q^{\prime}}
’s, we have
\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}=\sum_{q^{\prime}\in[q]}\#\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}}
and

r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}=\sum_{q^{\prime}\in[q]}r_{\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}}}
.

Since the graph
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\{0\}]
is connected, for all
q^{\prime}\in[q]
,
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\{0\}]
has an edge connecting
cc_{q^{\prime}}
to
S^{\prime}\cup\{0\}
. Thus, for all
q^{\prime}\in[q]
, either
supp(\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}})
intersects
supp(\alpha_{S^{\prime}})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}=supp(\alpha_{S^{\prime}})
, or that
col(\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}})
intersects
col(\alpha_{S^{\prime}})
. We deduce that

\#\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}}+r_{\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}}}\geq 1+|supp(\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}})\setminus supp(\alpha_{S^{\prime}})|+|col(\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}})\setminus col(\alpha_{S^{\prime}})|\kern 5.0pt.

Gathering everything, we get

\displaystyle\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}+\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}+\sum_{q^{\prime}\in[q]}\#\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}}+\sum_{q^{\prime}\in[q]}r_{\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}+\sum_{q^{\prime}\in[q]}1+|supp(\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}})\setminus supp(\alpha_{S^{\prime}})|+|col(\alpha_{cc_{q^{\prime}}})\setminus col(\alpha_{S^{\prime}})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\#\alpha_{S}+r_{\alpha_{S}}+cc\left(\mathcal{W}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)\kern 5.0pt,

which concludes the proof of the lemma.

D.6
Proof of Lemma
D.6

We proceed by induction on
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
to prove that

f(S)\leq|\alpha|^{|\alpha_{S}|-|supp(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-r_{\alpha_{S}}+2}\kern 5.0pt.

(71)

Since when
S\neq\emptyset
, we have
|supp(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|=|supp(\alpha_{S})|=\#\alpha_{s}
–see the remark below the definition of
\mathcal{S}([l])
, this is sufficient for our purpose.

The initialization is trivial since
f(\emptyset)=1
and
\alpha_{\emptyset}=0
. Let us take
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
and let us suppose that the result holds for all
S^{\prime}\subsetneq S
. For all
s\in S
, denote
S^{*}(s)
the maximal element of
\mathcal{S}([l])
which is included in
S\setminus\left\{s\right\}
. The existence of such an element in justified by the fact that the set of elements
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])
with
S^{\prime}\subseteq S\setminus\left\{s\right\}
is not empty (it contains
\emptyset
) and is stable by union. Then, we have

\displaystyle f(S)=

\displaystyle 1+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}f(S^{\prime})\leq 1+\sum_{s\in S}\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subseteq S^{*}(s)}}f(S^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{s\in S}\left[2f(S^{*}(s))-1\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{s\in S}f(S^{*}(s))\ ,

where we used the recursive definition of
f
in the second row. Applying the induction hypothesis leads us to

f(S)\leq 2\sum_{s\in S}|\alpha|^{|\alpha_{S^{*}(s)}|-|supp(\alpha_{S^{*}(s)})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-r_{\alpha_{S^{*}(s)}}+2}\kern 5.0pt.

(72)

Let us fix
s_{0}\in S\setminus S^{*}(s_{0})
. We denote in the following
S^{-}=S\setminus S^{*}(s_{0})
. Besides, let
S^{-}_{0}
denote the set of all
s\in S\setminus S^{*}(s_{0})
such that
j_{s}\in col(\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})})
. Then, we write
j^{1},\ldots,j^{r}\in[p]
the other columns of
S\setminus S^{*}(s_{0})
that do not appear in
col(\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})})
. Besides, for
r^{\prime}=1,\ldots,r
, we write
S^{-}_{r^{\prime}}
the set of
s\in S^{-}
such that
j_{s}=j^{r^{\prime}}
. Since the graph
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
is connected, we can suppose, up to some reordering, that for all
r^{\prime}\leq r
, there exists
i\in supp(\alpha_{S^{-}_{r^{\prime}}})\cap\left(supp\left(\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})}+\sum_{0\leq r^{\prime\prime}\leq r^{\prime}-1}\alpha_{S^{-}_{r^{\prime\prime}}}\right)\cup\left\{1,2\right\}\right)
. Moreover, at the final step
r^{\prime}=r
, from Lemma
D.4
, we know that there exist two distinct
i,i^{\prime}\in supp(\alpha_{S^{-}_{r}})\cap\left(supp\left(\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})}+\sum_{0\leq r^{\prime\prime}\leq r-1}\alpha_{S^{-}_{r^{\prime\prime}}}\right)\cup\left\{1,2\right\}\right)
. If
r\neq 0
, those two claims imply that
|\alpha_{S^{-}}|\geq r+1+|supp(\alpha_{S^{-}})\setminus\left(supp(\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}\right)|
. Since
\left\{1,2\right\}\subseteq supp(\alpha_{S})
, we derive from the latter that

|\alpha_{S}|-|supp(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-r_{\alpha_{S}}-1\geq|\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})}|-|supp(\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-r_{\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})}}\kern 5.0pt.

If
r=0
, we use the fact that all
i\in supp(\alpha_{S})
must satisfy
|\left(\alpha_{S}\right)_{i:}|\geq 2
–see Lemma
D.4
. This implies that

\displaystyle|\alpha_{S^{-}}|

\displaystyle\geq\min\left(2,2\left|supp(\alpha_{S^{-}})\setminus\left(supp(\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left|supp(\alpha_{S^{-}})\setminus\left(supp(\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}\right)\right|+1\kern 5.0pt.

Hence, as in the case
r\neq 0
, we also get in the case
r=0
that

|\alpha_{S}|-|supp(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-r_{\alpha_{S}}-1\geq|\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})}|-|supp(\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-r_{\alpha_{S^{*}(s_{0})}}\kern 5.0pt.

Hence, we deduce from (
72
) that

f(S)\leq\frac{2|S|}{|\alpha|}{|\alpha|}^{|\alpha_{S}|-|supp(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-r_{\alpha_{S}}+2}\leq{|\alpha|}^{|\alpha_{S}|-|supp(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-r_{\alpha_{S}}+2}\kern 5.0pt\ .

We have shown (
71
), which concludes the proof.

D.7
Proof of Lemma
D.2

In order to get a suitable upper bound of
|\kappa_{x,\alpha}|
from Theorem
2.5
and Lemma
D.1
, it is sufficient to upper-bound the number of partitions
\pi=\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{l}
of the multisets
\alpha
into groups of size
2
such that
C_{x,\beta_{1}(\pi),\ldots,\beta_{l}(\pi)}\neq 0
, where
\left(\beta_{s}(\pi)\right)_{ij}
counts the number of copies of
(i,j)
in
\pi_{s}
. Remember that for such a partition, it is necessary that, for
s\in[l]
,
\pi_{s}
is contained in a single column of
\alpha
. The number of partitions into pairings of each multiset
\alpha_{:j}
is at most
|\alpha_{:j}|^{\frac{|\alpha_{:j}|}{2}-1}
. We deduce that the total number of satisfying partitions is at most
|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-r_{\alpha}}
. Since
|\alpha|\geq 2\#\alpha
, we upper-bound this quantity by
|\alpha|^{|\alpha|-\#\alpha-r_{\alpha}}
.

Plugging this with Lemma
D.1
in Theorem
2.5
leads us to

|\kappa_{x,\alpha}|\leq\lambda^{|\alpha|}\rho^{r_{\alpha}}|\alpha|^{2\left(|\alpha|-r_{\alpha}-\#\alpha+2\right)}\min\left(\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-1},\frac{1}{K}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

which concludes the proof of the lemma.

D.8
Proof of Lemma
D.3

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}\neq 0
. We shall suppose successively that one of the conditions of Lemma
D.3
is not satisfied and and prove that
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.

1.

We suppose that either
\alpha_{1:}=0
or
\alpha_{2:}=0
. By symmetry, we suppose that
\alpha_{2:}=0
. Then, the label
k_{2}^{*}
is independent of the random variables
\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}\cup\left\{k^{*}_{1}\right\}
. And since
k^{*}_{2}
follows a uniform law on
[K]
, we directly deduce that
x=\mathbf{1}_{k_{1}^{*}=k_{2}^{*}}
is also independent from
\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}\cup\left\{k^{*}_{1}\right\}
(and a fortiori from
\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}
). By Lemma
B.2
, we have
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.

2.

We suppose that there exists
i_{0}\in supp(\alpha)
such that
\sum_{j\in[p]}\alpha_{i_{0}j}=1
and we shall prove that
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.
\varepsilon_{i_{0}}
is symmetric and independent from the other random variables. In particular,
x,\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}
has the same distribution as
x,\left(\left(-1\right)^{\mathbf{1}_{i=i_{0}}}X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}
and so
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=-\kappa_{x,\alpha}
. We deduce
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.

3.

We suppose that there exists
j_{0}\in[p]
such that
\sum_{i\in[n]}\alpha_{ij_{0}}=1
. It is clear in that case that there does not exist any decomposition
\alpha=\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}
with
\beta_{s}=\left\{(i_{s},j_{s});(i^{\prime}_{s},j_{s})\right\}
. Hence, Theorem
2.5
ensures that
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.

4.

Let us suppose that the graph
\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{-}\cup\left\{(u_{1},u_{2})\right\}
is not connected. Let
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}=\alpha
with
\beta_{s}=\left\{(i_{s},j_{s});(i^{\prime}_{s},j_{s})\right\}
. Let us prove that
C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}
is null. The fact that
\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}^{-}\cup\left\{(u_{1},u_{2})\right\}
is disconnected implies that the graph
\mathcal{W}
of
[0,l]
defined is Section
D.1
is also disconnected. We deduce from Lemma
D.4
that
C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}
is null. This being true for all decompositions, we conclude that
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.

E
Proof of Theorem
5.1

Theorem
5.1
states two LD lower bounds (
41
) and (
42
) in two different regimes. We prove them separately in Subsections
E.1
and prf:lowerboundbi1.

E.1
Reduction to a
L
-dimensional problem: Proof of (
42
)

Without loss of generality, we suppose that
\sigma^{2}=1
. Let us fix
D\in\mathbb{N}
and let us suppose that

\zeta^{\prime}:=\lambda^{4}D^{10}\frac{5p^{2}}{L}\max\left(1,\frac{n}{K^{2}}\right)<1\kern 5.0pt.

Working conditionally on
l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}
, we get

\displaystyle MMSE_{\leq D}=

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathbb{R}_{D}[Y]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(f(Y)-x\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathbb{R}_{D}[Y]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{l^{*}}\left[\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(f(Y)-x\right)^{2}|l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}}\left[\inf_{f\in\mathbb{R}_{D}[Y]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(f(Y)-x\right)^{2}|l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}\right]\right]\kern 5.0pt.

In the following, we fix
l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}
and we consider

MMSE_{\leq D}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=\inf_{f\in\mathbb{R}_{D}[Y]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(f(Y)-x\right)^{2}|l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Let us suppose that, for all
l\in[L]
, we have,
\left|\left\{j\in[p],l_{j}^{*}=l\right\}\right|\leq 5\frac{p}{L}
. We write

MMSE_{\leq D}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=\frac{1}{K}-corr_{\leq D}^{2}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})\kern 5.0pt.

We shall upper-bound
corr_{\leq D}^{2}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})
using Proposition
2.1
which states that

corr_{\leq D}^{2}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})\leq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}}\frac{\kappa_{x,\alpha}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})^{2}}{\alpha!}\kern 5.0pt,

where
\kappa_{x,\alpha}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}|l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}\right)
, where we see
\alpha
as a multiset of
[n]\times[p]
. This conditional biclustering model is a special case of the latent model (
1
) with

Z=(k^{*},l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}),\quad\delta_{ij}(k^{*})=\varepsilon^{r}_{i}\varepsilon^{c}_{j},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\theta_{i,j}(k^{*})=(k^{*}_{i},l^{*}_{j})\kern 5.0pt,

where
l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}
are considered as deterministic. Combining Proposition
2.1
and Theorem
2.5
, we need to upper-bound, for any multiset
\alpha
and any decomposition
\alpha=\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}
with
|\beta_{s}|=2
, the cumulant

C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\prod_{ij\in\beta_{1}}\varepsilon^{r}_{i}\varepsilon^{c}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\beta_{1}}(k^{*},l^{*})},\ldots,\prod_{ij\in\beta_{l}}\varepsilon^{r}_{i}\varepsilon^{c}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\beta_{l}}(k^{*},l^{*})}\Big|\kern 5.0ptl^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

with
\Omega_{\beta}(k^{*},l^{*}):=\left\{\big|\left\{(k^{*}_{i},l^{*}_{j}):\kern 5.0pt(i,j)\in\beta\right\}\big|=1\right\}
. Building on Lemma
C.1
, we derive the following upper-bound, whose proof is postponed to the end of the section.

Lemma E.1
.

We recall that
\#\alpha
stands for the cardinality of the points
i\in[n]
such that
\alpha_{i:}\neq 0
and that
|\alpha|:=\sum_{ij}\alpha_{ij}
. We have, for all
l^{*}
,
\varepsilon^{r}
,
\varepsilon^{c}
, that

|C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})|\leq|\alpha|^{|\alpha|}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha-1}\kern 5.0pt.

The number of partition of the multiset
\alpha
into groups of size
2
is at most
|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-1}\leq|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}
. Combining this with Lemma
E.1
and Theorem
2.5
leads us to

|\kappa_{x,\alpha}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})|\leq|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}|\alpha|^{|\alpha|}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha-1}\kern 5.0pt.

(73)

Then, we prune the multisets
\alpha
for which
\kappa_{x,\alpha}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=0
.

Lemma E.2
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}
be non-zero. If
\kappa_{x,\alpha}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})\neq 0
, then:

1.

1,2\in supp(\alpha)
;

2.

All the elements
i\in supp(\alpha)\setminus\left\{1,2\right\}
are such that
|\alpha_{i:}|\geq 2
;

3.

There exists a decomposition
\alpha=\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}
, where
\beta_{s}=\left\{(i_{s},j_{s}),(i^{\prime}_{s},j^{\prime}_{s})\right\}
, and such that, for all
s\in[l]
,
l^{*}_{j_{s}}=l^{*}_{j^{\prime}_{s}}
.

In particular, we have
\#\alpha\geq 2
and
|\alpha|\geq 2\#\alpha-2
.

The last step of the proof amounts to counting the number of
\alpha
’s satisfying the conditions of Lemma
E.2
.

Lemma E.3
.

Suppose that, for all
l\in[L]
, we have
\left|\left\{j\in[p],l_{j}^{*}=l\right\}\right|\leq 5\frac{p}{L}
. Let
d\in[D]
and
m\in[2,\frac{d+2}{2}]
. Then, there are at most
d^{2d}n^{m-2}\left(5\frac{p^{2}}{L}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}
matrices
\alpha
satisfying the conditions of Lemma
E.2
with
|\alpha|=d
and
\#\alpha=m
.

Combining Lemma
E.3
and (
73
), and supposing that, for all
l\in[L]
, we have
\left|\left\{j\in[p],l_{j}^{*}=l\right\}\right|\leq 5\frac{p}{L}
, we end up with

\displaystyle corr^{2}_{\leq D}-\frac{1}{K^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{d=1}^{D}\sum_{m\in[2,\frac{d+2}{2}]}d^{5d}\lambda^{2d}\left(\frac{n}{K^{2}}\right)^{m-2}\left(5\frac{p^{2}}{L}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{d=1}^{D}\sum_{m\in[2,\frac{d+2}{2}]}\left(\sqrt{\frac{5p^{2}}{L}}\lambda^{2}d^{5}\right)^{d}\left(\frac{n}{K^{2}}\right)^{m-2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{d=1}^{D}\sum_{m\in[2,\frac{d+2}{2}]}\left(\sqrt{\frac{5p^{2}}{L}}\lambda^{2}D^{5}\right)^{d-2(m-2)}\left(\lambda^{4}D^{10}\frac{5p^{2}n}{LK^{2}}\right)^{m-2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{d=1}^{D}\frac{d}{2}\zeta^{\prime d/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{\zeta^{\prime}}}{(1-\sqrt{\zeta^{\prime}})^{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Hence, provided that ,for all
l\in[L]
,
\left|\left\{j\in[p],l_{j}^{*}=l\right\}\right|\leq 5\frac{p}{L}
, we have

MMSE_{\leq D}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon_{c})\geq\frac{1}{K}-\frac{1}{K^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{\zeta^{\prime}}}{(1-\sqrt{\zeta^{\prime}})^{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Moreover, using a large deviation Inequality for Binomial random variables –see e.g Exercise 12.9.7 of
[
34
]
–, we have that, with probability at least
1-L\exp\left(-\frac{5p}{2L}\log(5)\right)
, for all
l\in[L]
,
\left|\left\{j\in[p],l_{j}^{*}=l\right\}\right|\leq 5\frac{p}{L}
. We deduce from this that

MMSE_{\leq D}\geq\left(1-L\exp\left(-\frac{5p}{2L}\log(5)\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{K}-\frac{1}{K^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{\zeta^{\prime}}}{(1-\sqrt{\zeta^{\prime}})^{2}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Let
\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}
such that
|\beta_{s}|=2
for
s\in[l]
and such that
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}=\alpha
and let
l^{*}\in[L]^{p}
. We seek to upper-bound

C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\prod_{ij\in\beta_{1}}\varepsilon^{r}_{i}\varepsilon^{c}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\beta_{1}}(k^{*},l^{*})},\ldots,\prod_{ij\in\beta_{l}}\varepsilon^{r}_{i}\varepsilon^{c}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\beta_{l}}(k^{*},l^{*})}\Big|\kern 5.0ptl^{*}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

with
\Omega_{\beta}(k^{*},l^{*}):=\left\{\big|\left\{(k^{*}_{i},l^{*}_{j}):\kern 5.0pt(i,j)\in\beta\right\}\big|=1\right\}
. For
C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}(l^{*})
to be non-zero, it is necessary that, for all
s\in[l]
,
\Omega_{\beta_{s}}(k^{*},l^{*})
is an event of positive probability conditionally on
l^{*}
. This condition enforces that
|\left\{l^{*}_{j}\right\}_{j\in col(\beta_{s})}|=1
for all
s
. We can assume that the latter property is true in the followsing.
We write
\beta_{s}=\left\{(i_{s},j_{s});(i^{\prime}_{s},j^{\prime}_{s})\right\}
, for
s\in[l]
, with
l^{*}_{j_{s}}=l^{*}_{j^{\prime}_{s}}
. We also take the convention
i_{0}=1
,
i^{\prime}_{0}=2
, and
j_{0}=0
. We then have

C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=\left(\prod_{s\in[l]}\prod_{ij\in\beta_{s}}\varepsilon^{r}_{i}\varepsilon^{c}_{j}\right)\mathrm{Cum}\left(\mathbf{1}\left\{k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right\}_{s\in[0,l]}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

which, in turn, implies

\left|C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})\right|=\left|\mathrm{Cum}\left(\mathbf{1}\left\{k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right\}_{s\in[0,l]}\right)\right|\kern 5.0pt.

From Lemma
C.1
, we deduce

|C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})|\leq|\alpha|^{|\alpha|}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha-1}\kern 5.0pt,

which concludes the proof of the lemma.

∎

1.

By symmetry, let us suppose that
1\notin supp(\alpha)
. Then, conditionally on
l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c}
,
x
is independent from
\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}
. We deduce from Lemma
B.2
that
\kappa_{x,\alpha}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=0
.

2.

Let us suppose that there exists
\underline{i}\in supp(\alpha)\setminus\left\{1,2\right\}
with
|\alpha_{\underline{i}:}|=1
. Consiser any decomposition
\alpha=\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}
with
\beta_{s}=\left\{(i_{s},j_{s});(i^{\prime}_{s},j^{\prime}_{s})\right\}
. Let
s_{0}
be the only element such that
\underline{i}\in supp(\beta_{s_{0}})
. It follows that
\mathbf{1}\{k^{*}_{i_{s_{0}}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s_{0}}}\}
is independent of
(x,(\mathbf{1}\{k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\})_{s\in[l]\setminus\left\{s_{0}\right\}})
and thus, from Lemma
B.2
, we deduce
C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=0
. This being true for all decompositions of
\alpha
, we have
\kappa_{x,\alpha}(l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c})=0
.

3.

The last point of the lemma is a direct consequence of Theorem
2.5
.

∎

Since we necessarily have
1,2\in supp(\alpha)
, there are at most
n^{m-2}
possibilities for choosing
supp(\alpha)
. Using the third point of Lemma
E.2
together with the hypothesis
\left|\left\{j\in[p],l_{j}^{*}=l\right\}\right|\leq 5\frac{p}{L}
, for all
l\in[L]
, we deduce that the number of possibilities for choosing
col(\alpha)
is at most
\left(\frac{5p^{2}}{L}\right)^{d/2}
. Finally, there are at most
m^{d}|col(\alpha)|^{d}\leq d^{2d}
possibilities for choosing
\alpha
once
supp(\alpha)
and
col(\alpha)
is determined. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

E.2
Proof of the first lower bound (
41
) of
MMSE_{\leq D}

Without loss of generality, we suppose through the proof that
\sigma^{2}=1
. Let us fix
D\in\mathbb{N}
and let us suppose

\zeta:=\lambda^{4}D^{8}\max\left(n,p,\frac{np}{K^{2}},\frac{np}{L^{2}}\right)<1\kern 5.0pt.

As in the proof of Theorems
3.1
and
4.1
, the expression of the
MMSE_{\leq D}
can be reduced to

MMSE_{\leq D}=\inf_{f\in\mathbb{R}_{D}[Y]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(f(Y)-x\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{K}-corr_{\leq D}^{2}\kern 5.0pt,

with
x=\mathbf{1}_{k^{*}_{1}=k^{*}_{2}}
and
corr_{\leq D}^{2}
being defined in Equation (
10
). We shall, as in the proofs of Theorem
3.1
and
4.1
, upper-bound
corr^{2}_{\leq D}
using Proposition
2.1
, which states that

corr_{\leq D}^{2}\leq\underset{|\alpha|\leq D}{\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}}}\frac{\kappa_{x,\alpha}^{2}}{\alpha!}\kern 5.0pt,

with
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}\right)
, where we see
\alpha
as a multiset of
[n]\times[p]
. The biclustering model is a special case of the latent model (
1
), with

Z=k^{*},l^{*},\varepsilon^{r},\varepsilon^{c},\quad\delta_{ij}(k^{*})=\varepsilon^{r}_{i}\varepsilon^{c}_{j},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\theta_{i,j}(k^{*})=(k^{*}_{i},l^{*}_{j})\kern 5.0pt.

Combining Proposition
2.1
and Theorem
2.5
, we need to upper-bound, for any multiset
\alpha
and any decomposition
\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}=\alpha
, with
|\beta_{s}|=2
, the cumulant

C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}=\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\prod_{ij\in\beta_{1}}\varepsilon^{r}_{i}\varepsilon^{c}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\beta_{1}}(k^{*},l^{*})},\ldots,\prod_{ij\in\beta_{l}}\varepsilon^{r}_{i}\varepsilon^{c}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\beta_{l}}(k^{*},l^{*})}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

with
\Omega_{\beta}(k^{*},l^{*}):=\left\{\big|\left\{(k^{*}_{i},l^{*}_{j}):\kern 5.0pt(i,j)\in\beta\right\}\big|=1\right\}
. Building on the recursive Bound (
18
), we derive in Section
E.3
the following upper-bound.

Lemma E.4
.

We recall that
\#\alpha
stands for the cardinality of the points
i\in[1,n]
such that
\alpha_{i:}\neq 0
and that
|\alpha|:=\sum_{ij}\alpha_{ij}
. We have

|C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}|\leq|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}\frac{1}{K}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K\wedge L}\right)^{\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Combining this bound with (
55
) and counting the number of partitions
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\alpha)
for which
C_{x,\beta_{1}(\pi),\ldots,\beta_{l}(\pi)}\neq 0
, we prove in Section
E.8
the next upper-bound on
|\kappa_{x,\alpha}|
.

Lemma E.5
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}
non-zero. We have the upper bound

|\kappa_{x,\alpha}|\leq\lambda^{|\alpha|}|\alpha|^{|\alpha|}\frac{1}{K}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K\wedge L}\right)^{\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

The last stage, is to prune the multisets
\alpha
for which
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.
Next lemma gives necessary conditions for having
\kappa_{x,\alpha}\neq 0
. We refer to Section
E.9
for a proof of this lemma.

Lemma E.6
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}
be non-zero. If
\kappa_{x,\alpha}\neq 0
, then

•

1,2\in supp(\alpha)
;

•

For all
i\in supp(\alpha)
,
|\alpha_{i:}|\geq 2
;

•

For all
j\in col(\alpha)
,
|\alpha_{j:}|\geq 2
.

In particular, we have
\#\alpha\geq 2
,
|\alpha|\geq 2r_{\alpha}
and
|\alpha|\geq 2\#\alpha
.

For any
r\geq 1
,
m\geq 2
and
d\geq\max\left(2r,2m\right)
, there are at most
p^{r}n^{m-2}d^{2d}
matrices
\alpha
satisfying the conditions of Lemma
E.6
with
|\alpha|=d
,
r_{\alpha}=r
and
\#\alpha=m
. Using Proposition
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle corr^{2}_{\leq D}\leq

\displaystyle\underset{|\alpha|\leq D}{\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}}}\kappa_{x,\alpha}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}+\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{d=2}^{D}\sum_{m=2}^{d/2}\sum_{r=1}^{d/2}p^{r}n^{m-2}d^{4d}\lambda^{2d}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K\wedge L}\right)^{2m+2r-d-4}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Let us fix
r\geq 1
,
m\geq 2
and
d\geq\max\left(2r,2m\right)
and let us upper-bound
p^{r}n^{m-2}d^{4d}\lambda^{2d}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K\wedge L}\right)^{2m+2r-d-4}\right)
. First, we suppose that
d\geq 2\left(m-2+r\right)
and we get

\displaystyle p^{r}n^{m-2}d^{4d}\lambda^{2d}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K\wedge L}\right)^{2m+2r-d-4}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\left(D^{4}\lambda^{2}\right)^{d}p^{r}n^{m-2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(D^{4}\lambda^{2}\right)^{d-2r-2(m-2)}\left(D^{8}\lambda^{4}n\right)^{m-2}\left(D^{8}\lambda^{4}p\right)^{r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\zeta}^{d-2r-2(m-2)}\zeta^{m-2}\zeta^{r}=\zeta^{d/2}\kern 5.0pt.

Then, let us suppose that
d\leq 2\left(m-2+r\right)
. By symmetry of the roles of
K
and
L
, we suppose that
K\wedge L=K
. We get

\displaystyle p^{r}n^{m-2}d^{4d}\lambda^{2d}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K\wedge L}\right)^{2m+2r-d-4}\right)=

\displaystyle p^{r}n^{m-2}\left(2d^{4}\right)^{d}\lambda^{2d}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{2m+2r-d-4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p^{r}n^{m-2}\left(D^{4}\lambda^{2}\right)^{d}\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{2m+2r-d-4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n^{\frac{d}{2}-r}\left(\frac{n}{K^{2}}\right)^{m-2-\left(\frac{d}{2}-r\right)}p^{r}\left(D^{4}\lambda^{2}\right)^{2r+2(d/2-r)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(D^{8}\lambda^{4}p\right)^{r}\left(D^{8}\lambda^{4}n\right)^{\frac{d}{2}-r}\left(\frac{n}{K^{2}}\right)^{m-2-\left(\frac{d}{2}-r\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(D^{8}\lambda^{4}n\right)^{\frac{d}{2}-r}\left(D^{8}\lambda^{4}p\right)^{\frac{d}{2}-(m-2)}\left(D^{8}\lambda^{4}p\frac{n}{K^{2}}\right)^{m-2-\left(\frac{d}{2}-r\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\zeta}^{d}\kern 5.0pt.

In the end, we get

\displaystyle corr^{2}_{\leq D}-\frac{1}{K^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{d=2}^{D}\sum_{m=2}^{d/2}\sum_{r=1}^{d/2}\sqrt{\zeta}^{d}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\sum_{d=2}^{D}\frac{d(d-1)}{2}\sqrt{\zeta}^{d}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K^{2}}\frac{\zeta}{\left(1-\sqrt{\zeta}\right)^{3}}\kern 5.0pt,

which concludes the proof of the theorem.

E.3
Proof of Lemma
E.4

Let
\alpha=\beta_{1}+\ldots+\beta_{l}
with, for
s\in[l]
,
\beta_{s}=\left\{(i_{s},j_{s});(i^{\prime}_{s},j^{\prime}_{s})\right\}
. Let us upper-bound the absolute value of the cumulant

C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}=\mathrm{Cum}\left(x,\left(\varepsilon^{r}_{i_{s}}\varepsilon^{r}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\varepsilon^{c}_{j_{s}}\varepsilon^{c}_{j^{\prime}_{s}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\beta_{s}}(k^{*},l^{*})}\right)_{s\in[l]}\right).

For
S\subseteq[l]
, we write
\beta[S]:=\left\{\beta_{s},s\in S\right\}
and we write
\alpha_{S}=\sum_{s\in S}\beta_{s}
. In the following, we take the convention
i_{0}=1
,
i^{\prime}_{0}=2
and
j_{0}=0
. Applying the recursion formula
18
, we have, for all
S\subseteq[l]
,

\displaystyle|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in S\cup\left\{0\right\},k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right]\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in S,l^{*}_{j_{s}}=l^{*}_{j^{\prime}_{s}}\right]+

\displaystyle+\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}\left|C_{x,\beta[S^{\prime}]}\right|\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in S\setminus S^{\prime},k^{*}_{i_{s}}=k^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\right]\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\forall s\in S\setminus S^{\prime},l^{*}_{j_{s}}=l^{*}_{j^{\prime}_{s}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{|supp(\alpha_{S})\cup\left\{1,2\right\}|-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S]\right)}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}\left|C_{x,\beta[S^{\prime}]}\right|\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)}\kern 5.0pt,

(74)

where
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{N}
are two graphs on
[0,l]
defined as follows. For
s,s^{\prime}\in[0,l]
,
\mathcal{V}
has an edge between
s
and
s^{\prime}
if and only if
\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}\cap\left\{i_{s^{\prime}},i^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}\right\}\neq\emptyset
. For
s,s^{\prime}\in[0,l]
,
\mathcal{N}
has an edge between
s
and
s^{\prime}
if and only if
\left\{j_{s},j^{\prime}_{s}\right\}\cap\left\{j_{s^{\prime}},j^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}\right\}\neq\emptyset
(we consider
j_{0}=j^{\prime}_{0}=0
which implies that
0
is an isolated point of
\mathcal{N}
). We also define the graph
\mathcal{W}
on
[0,l]
with an edge between
s
and
s^{\prime}
if and only if either
\mathcal{V}
or
\mathcal{N}
has an edge between
s
and
s^{\prime}
. Finally, given a subset
S\subset[0,l]
, we define
\mathcal{V}(S)
,
\mathcal{N}(S)
, and
\mathcal{W}(S)
as the subgraphs of
\mathcal{V}
,
\mathcal{N}
, and
\mathcal{W}
induced by
S
.

The next lemma prunes the subsets
S\subseteq[l]
such that
C_{x,\beta[S]}\neq 0
. In the following, we denote
\mathcal{S}([l])
the collection of all subsets
S\subseteq[l]
such that either
S=\emptyset
or
\mathcal{W}[\left\{0\right\}\cup S]
is connected and both
1,2\in supp(\alpha_{S})
. We postpone to Section
E.4
the proof of the next lemma.

Lemma E.7
.

Let
S\subseteq[l]
such that
C_{x,\beta[S]}\neq 0
. Then
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
.

In particular, we can henceforth restrict our attention to subsets
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
, so that

\displaystyle|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S]\right)}+

\displaystyle+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}\left|C_{x,\beta[S^{\prime}]}\right|\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

(75)

In the following, let us define recursively a function
f
on
\mathcal{S}([l])
satisfying, for all
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
,

f(S)=1+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}f(S^{\prime})\kern 5.0pt.

(76)

In particular,
f(\emptyset)=1
. Using the connectivity of
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
whenever
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
is non-empty, we prove in Section
D.3
the following lemma.

Lemma E.8
.

For all
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
, we have
|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq f(S)\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S])}.

The next lemma, proved in Section
E.6
, provides an upper-bound of
f([l])

Lemma E.9
.

For all
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
, we have
f([l])\leq|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}}

Combining Lemma
E.9
and Lemma
E.8
implies that

|C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}|\leq\left(|\alpha|\right)^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}\frac{1}{K}\left(\frac{1}{K\wedge L}\right)^{\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-cc(\mathcal{V}[[0,l]])-cc(\mathcal{V}[[l]])-1}\kern 5.0pt.

(77)

It remains to lower-bound the quantity
\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-cc(\mathcal{V}[[0,l]])-cc(\mathcal{V}[[l]])
. To do so, we shall use the connectivity of the graph
\mathcal{W}[0,l]
. We postpone to Section
E.7
the proof of the next lemma.

Lemma E.10
.

We have

\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-cc(\mathcal{V}[[0,l]])-cc(\mathcal{V}[[l]])-1\geq\max\left(0,\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-2\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Using Lemma
E.10
, we are able to conclude the proof of the lemma with

|C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}|\leq|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}\frac{1}{K}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K\wedge L}\right)^{\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

E.4
Proof of Lemma
E.7

Let
S\notin\mathcal{S}([l])
and let us prove that
C_{x,\beta[S]}=0
.

Let us first suppose that
\mathcal{W}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
is not connected. Let
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
be a partition of
S\cup\left\{0\right\}
with no edges of
\mathcal{W}
connecting them. Hence, the family of random variables
((\varepsilon^{r}_{i},k^{*}_{i})_{i\in\cup_{s\in C_{1}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}},\left(\varepsilon^{c}_{j},l^{*}_{j}\right)_{j\in\cup_{s\in C_{1}}\left\{j_{s},j^{\prime}_{s}\right\}})
is independent of the family

((\varepsilon^{r}_{i},k^{*}_{i})_{i\in\cup_{s\in C_{2}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}},\left(\varepsilon^{c}_{j},l^{*}_{j}\right)_{j\in\cup_{s\in C_{2}}\left\{j_{s},j^{\prime}_{s}\right\}})
. Then, Lemma
B.2
implies that
C_{x,\beta_{[S]}}=0
.

Let us now suppose that either
1\notin supp(\alpha_{S})
either
2\notin supp(\alpha_{S})
. Then,
\mathbf{1}_{k^{*}_{1}=k^{*}_{2}}
is independent from
((\varepsilon^{r}_{i},k^{*}_{i})_{i\in\cup_{s\in S}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}},\left(\varepsilon^{c}_{j},l^{*}_{j}\right)_{j\in\cup_{s\in S}\left\{j_{s},j^{\prime}_{s}\right\}})
. Thus, from Lemma
B.2
, we deduce that
C_{x,\beta_{[S]}}=0
.

E.5
Proof of Lemma
E.8

Let us prove by induction on
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
that

|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq f(S)\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S])}\kern 5.0pt.

The initialization is straightforward since
f(\emptyset)=1
and
C_{x,\beta[\emptyset]}=\frac{1}{K}
. Consider a set
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
non-empty and let us suppose that the result holds for all
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])
with
S^{\prime}\subsetneq S
. Combining (
75
) and the induction hypothesis leads to

\displaystyle|C_{x,\beta[S]}|\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S]\right)}+

\displaystyle+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}\left|C_{x,\beta[S^{\prime}]}\right|\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S]\right)}+\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S]\right)}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{s}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S]\right)}+

\displaystyle+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{\emptyset\neq S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}f(S^{\prime})\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S^{\prime}\cup\left\{0\right\}])-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}+r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S^{\prime}]\right)-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

Let us deal with the term corresponding to
S^{\prime}=\emptyset
. It is clear that
cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S]\right)\leq cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]\right)+1
. Thus
\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S]\right)}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{s}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S]\right)}\leq\left(\frac{1}{K}\right)^{\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])}\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{r_{\alpha_{S}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S]\right)}
.

By the recursive definition of
f(S)
, it is sufficient to prove that, for all non empty
S^{\prime}\subsetneq S
, we both have

\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S^{\prime}\cup\left\{0\right\}])-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}])\geq\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])

and

r_{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}}+r_{\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S^{\prime}]\right)-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S\setminus S^{\prime}]\right)\geq r_{\alpha_{S}}-cc\left(\mathcal{N}[S]\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Let us prove only that
\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S^{\prime}\cup\left\{0\right\}])-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}])\geq\#\alpha_{S}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])
, the proof being similar for the other term. Let
q=cc(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}])
and let
cc_{1},\ldots,cc_{q}
be the connected components of
\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]
. Let
q^{\prime}\in[q]
and let
h=cc(\mathcal{V}[\left(S^{\prime}\cup\left\{0\right\}\right)\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}])+cc(\mathcal{V}[(S\setminus S^{\prime})\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}])
. Let
a_{1},\ldots,a_{h}
denote the collection of those connected components. Since the graph
\mathcal{V}[cc_{q^{\prime}}]
is connected, we can, up to a possible reordering of these connected components, suppose that, for all
h^{\prime}\in[2,h]
,
\cup_{s\in a_{h^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}
intersects
\cup_{h^{\prime\prime}<h^{\prime}}\cup_{s\in a_{h^{\prime\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}
. We deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{h^{\prime}\in[h]}|\cup_{s\in a_{h^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|=

\displaystyle|\cup_{s\in a_{1}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|+\sum_{h^{\prime}\geq 2}|\cup_{s\in a_{h^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|\cup_{s\in a_{1}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|+\sum_{h^{\prime}\geq 2}\left(1+\left|\cup_{s\in a_{h^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}\setminus\left(\cup_{h^{\prime\prime}<h^{\prime}}\cup_{s\in a_{h^{\prime\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|\cup_{s\in cc_{q^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|+cc(\mathcal{V}[S^{\prime}\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}])+cc(\mathcal{V}[(S\setminus S^{\prime})\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}])-1\kern 5.0pt.

Together with the fact that
\sum_{h^{\prime}\in[h]}|\cup_{s\in a_{h^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|=|\cup_{s\in\left(S^{\prime}\cup\left\{0\right\}\right)\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|+|\cup_{s\in(S^{\prime}\setminus S)\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|
, this leads us to

\displaystyle|\cup_{s\in\left(S^{\prime}\cup\left\{0\right\}\right)\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|+|\cup_{s\in(S\setminus S^{\prime})\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|-cc(\mathcal{V}[S^{\prime}\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}])-cc(\mathcal{V}[(S\setminus S^{\prime})\cap cc_{q^{\prime}}])

\displaystyle\geq|\cup_{s\in cc_{q^{\prime}}}\left\{i_{s},i^{\prime}_{s}\right\}|-1\kern 5.0pt.

Summing other all
q^{\prime}\in[q]
leads us to

\#\alpha_{S^{\prime}}+\#\alpha_{S\setminus S^{\prime}}-cc(\mathcal{V}[S^{\prime}\cup\left\{0\right\}])-cc(\mathcal{V}[S\setminus S^{\prime}])\geq\#\alpha_{S}-cc\left(\mathcal{V}[S\cup\left\{0\right\}]\right)\kern 5.0pt.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

E.6
Proof of Lemma
E.9

We proceed by induction to prove that, for all
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
, we have

f(S)\leq|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}}
.
The initialization is trivial since
f(\emptyset)=1
and
\alpha_{\emptyset}=0
. Let us take
S\in\mathcal{S}([l])
non empty and let us suppose that the result holds for all
S^{\prime}\subsetneq S
. For all
s\in S
, let
S^{*}(s)
the maximal element of
\mathcal{S}([l])
which is included in
S\setminus\left\{s\right\}
. The existence of such an element in provided from the fact that the set of elements
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])
with
S^{\prime}\subseteq S\setminus\left\{s\right\}
is not empty (it contains
\emptyset
) and is stable by union. We have

\displaystyle f(S)=1+\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S}}f(S^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{s\in S}\underset{S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}([l])}{\sum_{S^{\prime}\subsetneq S^{*}(s)}}f(S^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{s\in S}\left[2f(S^{*}(s))-1\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{s\in S}f(S^{*}(s)).

Applying the induction hypothesis leads to

f(S)\leq 2\sum_{s\in S}|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha_{S^{*}(s)}|}{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Since
S^{*}(s)
does not contain
s
, it follows that
|\alpha_{S^{*}_{s}}|\leq|\alpha_{S}|-2
. We deduce that

f(S)\leq 2\sum_{s\in S}|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}-1}=\frac{2|S|}{|\alpha|}|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}}\leq\left(|\alpha|\right)^{\frac{|\alpha_{S}|}{2}}\kern 5.0pt,

where the last inequality comes from the fact that
|S|\leq\frac{|\alpha|}{2}
. This concludes the induction and the proof of the lemma.

E.7
Proof of Lemma
E.10

Recall that
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}[[0,l]]
and that
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{W}[[0,l]]
. For short, we write
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}[[l]]
.

We know that
\left\{1,2\right\}\subset supp(\alpha)
and that they are in the same connected component of
\mathcal{V}
. Thus, it is clear that
\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-cc(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})-cc(\mathcal{V})\geq 1
. It remains to prove that

\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-cc(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})-cc(\mathcal{V})\geq\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-1\kern 5.0pt,

which is equivalent to

|\alpha|\geq 2\left(cc(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})+cc(\mathcal{V})-2\right)\kern 5.0pt.

We shall use the fact that
\mathcal{W}
is connected. We write
q=cc(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})
. We write
J_{1},\ldots,J_{q}
the partition of
col(\alpha)
induced by the equivalence relation;
j
and
j^{\prime}
are equivalent if and only if there exists
s,s^{\prime}
in the same connected component of
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
such that
j\in col(\beta_{s})
and
j^{\prime}\in col(\beta_{s^{\prime}})
. For
R\subseteq[l]
, we write
q(R)\subseteq[q]
the collection of
q^{\prime}
such that
\sum_{s\in R}\sum_{j\in J_{q^{\prime}}}|(\beta_{s})_{:j}|\neq 0
. In other words,
q(R)
also corresponds to the collection of connected components of
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
that intersect with
R
.
Let us finally write
t=cc(\mathcal{V})
and
cc_{1},\ldots,cc_{t}
the connected components of
\mathcal{V}
.

The graph
\mathcal{W}
corresponds to the superposition of
\mathcal{V}
and of
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
. Connected components in
\mathcal{V}
are connected by edges in
\mathcal{N}
.

Besides, recall that
\mathcal{W}
is connected. Hence, we can assume, without loss of generality, that for all
t^{\prime}\in[2,t]
,
q(cc_{t^{\prime}}\setminus\left\{0\right\})
intersects
\cup_{t^{\prime\prime}\leq t^{\prime}-1}q(cc_{t^{\prime\prime}}\setminus\left\{0\right\})
. For all
t^{\prime}\in[t]
, it is clear that
|\alpha_{cc_{t^{\prime}}\setminus\left\{0\right\}}|\geq 2|q(cc_{t^{\prime}}\setminus\left\{0\right\})|
. Hence, we conclude that

\displaystyle|\alpha|\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{t^{\prime}\leq t}|\alpha_{cc_{t^{\prime}}\setminus\left\{0\right\}}|\geq\sum_{t^{\prime}\leq t}2q(cc_{t^{\prime}}\setminus\left\{0\right\})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{t^{\prime}\leq t}\left(\mathbf{1}\left\{t^{\prime}\neq 1\right\}+\left|q(cc_{t^{\prime}}\setminus\left\{0\right\})\setminus\cup_{t^{\prime\prime}\leq t^{\prime}-1}q(cc_{t^{\prime\prime}}\setminus\left\{0\right\})\right|\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2(t-1)+2\left|q([l])\right|=2t+2q-1\kern 5.0pt.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

E.8
Proof of Lemma
E.5

In order to get a satisfying upper-bound of
|\kappa_{x,\alpha}|
from Inequality Theorem
2.5
and Lemma
E.4
, it is sufficient to remark that
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\alpha)
contains at most
|\alpha|^{\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-1}
elements.

Plugging this with Lemma
E.4
in Theorem
2.5
leads to

|\kappa_{x,\alpha}|\leq|\alpha|\frac{1}{K}\min\left(1,\left(\frac{1}{K\wedge L}\right)^{\#\alpha+r_{\alpha}-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

which concludes the proof of the lemma.

E.9
Proof of Lemma
E.6

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n\times p}
non-zero. Let us prove that if
\alpha
does not satisfy the three conditions of Lemma
E.6
, then
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.

First, we suppose that either
1
or
2
is not in
supp(\alpha)
. This implies that
x
is independent from
\left(X_{ij}\right)_{ij\in\alpha}
and we deduce from Lemma
B.2
that
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.

Let us suppose that there exists
i_{0}\in supp(\alpha)
with
|\alpha_{i_{0}:}|=1
. Since
\varepsilon^{r}_{i_{0}}
has the same law as
-\varepsilon^{r}_{i_{0}}
and is independent from all the other variables (in particular, changing
\varepsilon^{r}_{i_{0}}
by
-\varepsilon^{r}_{i_{0}}
does not change the law of
(x,X)
). So, by multilinearity of cumulants, we have
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=\left(-1\right)^{|\alpha_{i_{0}:}|}\kappa_{x,\alpha}=-\kappa_{x,\alpha}
. We deduce directly that
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.

Similarly, if there exists
j_{0}
such that
|\alpha|_{:j_{0}}=1
, we use the fact that changing
\varepsilon^{c}_{j_{0}}
to
-\varepsilon^{c}_{j_{0}}
does not change the law of
(x,X)
and we deduce
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=\left(-1\right)^{|\alpha_{:j_{0}}|}\kappa_{x,\alpha}=-\kappa_{x,\alpha}
so that
\kappa_{x,\alpha}=0
.

F
Proof of Corollary
2.6

To get the Bound (
19
), we start from Möbius formula – see Lemma
B.1
in Appendix
B
–

\displaystyle|C_{x,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{l}}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}\left([l]\cup\left\{x\right\}\right)}(|\pi|-1)!\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[|x|;\underset{s\in\pi_{1}\setminus\left\{x\right\}}{\cap}\Omega_{\beta_{s}}\right]\prod_{k=2}^{|\pi|}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\underset{s\in\pi_{k}}{\cap}\Omega_{\beta_{s}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}\left([l]\cup\left\{x\right\}\right)}\left\{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\left[|x|;\underset{s\in\pi_{1}\setminus\left\{x\right\}}{\cap}\Omega_{\beta_{s}}\right]\prod_{k=2}^{|\pi|}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\underset{s\in\pi_{k}}{\cap}\Omega_{\beta_{s}}\right]\right\}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}\left([l]\cup\left\{x\right\}\right)}(|\pi|-1)!\kern 5.0pt.

Denoting by
{l+1\brace k}
the Stirling number of the second kind, which counts the number of partitions
\pi\in\mathcal{P}\left([l]\cup\left\{x\right\}\right)
with
k
non-empty sets, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}\left([l]\cup\left\{x\right\}\right)}(|\pi|-1)!

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{l+1}{l+1\brace k}(k-1)!

\displaystyle=l!+{l+1\brace l}(l-1)!+\sum_{k=1}^{l-1}{l\brace k}k!+\sum_{k=2}^{l-1}{l\brace k-1}(k-1)!

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{l}{l\brace k}k!+\sum_{k=1}^{l-2}{l\brace k}k!+{l+1\brace l}(l-1)!

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{l}{l\brace k}k!=2f_{l},

where we used for the penultimate equality that

{l+1\brace l}(l-1)!={(l+1)\over 2}l!={(l-1)\over 2}l!+l!={l\brace l-1}(l-1)!+l!\kern 5.0pt.

The bound on
f_{l}
readily follows from the exponential generating function evaluated at
x=1/2

\sum_{l\geq 1}{f_{l}\over l!}\left(1\over 2\right)^{l}={1\over 2-\exp(1/2)}\leq 3.

The proof of Corollary
2.6
is complete.

G
Proof of the upper bounds

G.1
Proof of Proposition
3.2

Let us suppose without loss of generality that
\sigma^{2}=1
. We shall introduce two different procedures corresponding to the different regimes.

Both procedures proceed from the same general scheme:

1.

We split the dataset randomly into two datasets
Y^{(1)}
and
Y^{(2)}
;

2.

We compute
\hat{v}_{1},\ldots,\hat{v}_{K}
the leading eigenvectors of
\left(Y^{(1)}\right)^{T}Y^{(1)}
and we project orthogonally
Y^{(2)}
onto
\hat{v}_{1},\ldots,\hat{v}_{K}
;

3.

We apply a low-dimensional clustering procedure on the projected dataset
\hat{p}\left(Y^{(2)}\right)
;

4.

We perform Linear Discriminant Analysis in order to assign each point of
Y^{(1)}
to one of the clusters of
\hat{p}\left(Y^{(2)}\right)
.

Let
\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{n}
i.i.d uniformly taken on
\left\{1,2\right\}
. Let
I_{1}=\left\{i\in[n],\delta_{i}=1\right\}
and
I_{2}=\left\{i\in[n],\delta_{i}=2\right\}
. Let
Y^{(1)}\in\mathbb{R}^{|I_{1}|\times p}
be the data matrix restricted to
I_{1}
and
Y^{(2)}\in\mathbb{R}^{|I_{2}|\times p}
be the date matrix restricted to
I_{2}
. Let
\hat{v}_{1},\ldots,\hat{v}_{K}
be the
K
leading eigenvectors of
\left(Y^{(1)}\right)^{T}Y^{(1)}
and let
\hat{p}
be the orthogonal projection on
\hat{v}_{1},\ldots,\hat{v}_{K}
. The following key lemma ensures that the projected centers are still well-separated.

We recall that, in this section, we assume that the partition
G^{*}
is balanced in the following sense.
For some constant
\gamma>0
,we have

\frac{\max_{k}|G_{k}^{*}|}{\min_{k}|G_{k}^{*}|}\leq\gamma\kern 5.0pt.

Lemma G.1
.

We suppose
\max(K,\log(n))\leq p\leq n
. There exists a constants
c_{\gamma}
that only depends on
\gamma
such that the following holds provided that
\Delta^{4}\geq c_{\gamma}\frac{pK^{2}}{n}
. With probability at least
1-\frac{4}{n^{2}}
, we have
\|\hat{p}(\mu_{k})-\hat{p}(\mu_{l})\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}\|\mu_{k}-\mu_{l}\|^{2}
for all
k,l\in[1,K]
.

We organize the proof in the following way. In Section
G.1.1
, we apply
[
49
]
to the projected dataset
Y^{(2)}
this allows to prove the second part Proposition
3.2
–see Proposition
G.3
below. In Section
G.1.2
, we apply some hierarchical clustering procedure which will lead to the first part of Proposition
3.2
–see Proposition
G.6
. Finally, in Section
G.1.3
, we provide a proof of Lemma
G.1
.

Throughout the proof, we shall multiple times rely on the following lemma that ensures that the restrictions of
G^{*}
to
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
are balanced.

Lemma G.2
.

Suppose that
n\geq c\gamma^{2}K^{2}
with
c
a numerical constant. Then, with probability higher than
1-1/n^{2}
, for all
k\in[K]
, we have
|G_{k}^{*}\cap I_{1}|\geq|G_{k}^{*}|/4
and
|G_{k}^{*}\cap I_{2}|\geq|G_{k}^{*}|/4
.

In the following, we work conditionally on
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
and we assume, without loss of generality, that the event of Lemma
G.2
holds.

Let us fix
k\in[K]
and let us consider
|G_{k}^{*}\cap I_{1}|
which is a binomial of parameters
|G_{k}^{*}|\geq\frac{n}{K\gamma}
and
\frac{1}{2}
. Using Hoeffding Inequality, we deduce that, for
t>0
,

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[||G_{k}^{*}\cap I_{1}|-\frac{|G_{k}^{*}|}{2}|\geq t\right]\leq 2\exp\left(\frac{-2t^{2}}{|G_{k}^{*}|}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Taking
t=\frac{|G_{k}^{*}|}{4}
, applying an union bound on all
k\in[K]
, yields that the desired result holds with probabilily higher than
1-2K\exp[-(\min_{k=1,\ldots,K}|G_{k}^{*}|)/4]
which is larger than
1-1/n
as soon since
|G_{k}^{*}|\geq n/[K\gamma]\geq c^{1/2}\sqrt{n}
.
∎

G.1.1
Tensor method of Li and Liu
[
49
]

In this section, we apply as a black-box the iterative tensor projection procedure of
[
49
]
to the projected dataset
\hat{p}\left(Y^{(2)}\right)
. This polynomial-time method is described in Algorithm
1
. In this subsection, we denote
\hat{G}
the resulting estimator of the partition.

Data:

Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}

Draw
(\delta_{i})_{i\in[1,n]}
independently and uniformly on
\{1,2\}
;

Compute
\hat{v_{1}},\ldots,\hat{v}_{K}
the leading eigenvectors of
\left(Y^{(1)}\right)^{T}Y^{(1)}
, with
Y^{(1)}
the restriction of
Y
to
I_{1}
;

For
i\in I_{2}
, compute
\hat{p}(Y_{i})
the orthogonal projection of
Y_{i}
on the space
Vect\left(\hat{v_{1}},\ldots,\hat{v}_{K}\right)
(if
p\leq\max\left(K,\log(n)\right)
keep
Y_{i}
);

Compute
\hat{G}
the clustering of the projected dataset
\hat{p}(Y^{(2)})
using the method from
[
49
]
;

for

k\in[1,K]

do

Compute
\hat{\mu}_{k}:=\frac{1}{|\hat{G}_{k}|}\sum_{i\in\hat{G}_{k}}Y_{i}
end for

for

i\in I_{1}

do

Assign
i
to the group
\hat{G}_{k}
minimizing
\|Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}_{k}\|
. end for

Result:

The partition
\hat{G}
.

Algorithm 1

Projection and iterative tensor projection

The following proposition states that
\hat{G}
perfectly recover the unknown partition provided the separation
\Delta^{2}
is large compared to
\log(n)+\sqrt{\frac{pK^{2}}{n}}
. This comes to the price of the condition that
n
is at least polynomial in
K
.

Proposition G.3
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exist constants
c_{\gamma}
,
c^{\prime}_{\gamma,\epsilon}>0
, and
c_{2}>0
such that the following holds. If
\Delta^{2}\geq c_{\gamma}\left(\log(n)^{1+\varepsilon}+\sqrt{\frac{pK^{2}}{n}}\right)
and
n\geq K^{c^{\prime}_{\gamma,\epsilon}}
, the output
\hat{G}
of Algorithm
1
satisfies

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\hat{G}=G^{*}\right]\geq 1-c_{2}/n^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

This proof mostly builds upon Lemma
G.1
and the work of
[
49
]
.
Note that if
p\leq\max\left(\log(n),K\right)
, one does not need to use the projection
\hat{p}
and we can consider
\hat{p}=I_{d}
. In all cases, the dimension of the projected dataset is at most
\max\left(\log(n),K\right)
.

We work conditionally on
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
. Without loss of generality, this event being of high probability when
n\geq c_{\gamma}K^{2}
, we suppose that
|I_{1}|,|I_{2}|\geq n/4
and that for all
k\in[K]
,
|G_{k}^{*}\cap I_{1}|,|G_{k}^{*}\cap I_{2}|\geq\frac{|G_{k}^{*}|}{4}
(Lemma
G.2
). Conditionally on
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, the dataset
Y^{(2)}
is independent from
Y^{(1)}
and thus
Y^{(2)}
is independent from
\hat{p}
. We then deduce from Lemma
G.1
that
\hat{p}(Y^{(2)})
is a Gaussian mixture with
K
groups of dimension at most
\max\left(\log(n),K\right)
and, with high probability, with a separatition at least
\Delta/2
between the groups. Then, we are in position to
the results in Section 2.2 of
[
49
]
that we state here as a Proposition. In fact, the original theorem of Li and Liu is stated for a Gaussian mixture model where the the group of each observation is sampled at random, whereas we are considering here a setting where the partition
G^{*}
is fixed in advance. Nevertheless, by closely inspecting the proof, one readily checks that their result extends to our setting.

Proposition G.4
.

[
49
]

Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p^{\prime}}
being sampled from a mixture of Gaussian with an almost balanced partition
G^{\prime*}
(i.e for all
k\in[K]
,
|G^{\prime*}_{k}|\geq\frac{n^{\prime}}{\gamma 2K}
) of
[n^{\prime}]
and centers
\mu^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mu^{\prime}_{K}\in\mathbb{R}^{p^{\prime}}
. For all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists positive constant
c_{\gamma}
,
c^{\prime}_{\gamma,\epsilon}
, and
c^{\prime\prime}
such that the following holds. If
\min_{k\neq l}\|\mu^{\prime}_{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{j}\|\geq c^{\prime}\left(\log(n)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}
and
n^{\prime}\geq(p^{\prime}K)^{c}
, there exists an algorithm
\hat{G}
computable in polynomial time such that

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left[\hat{G}=G\right]\geq 1-1/n^{c^{\prime\prime}}\kern 5.0pt.

Suppose that
c_{\gamma}
in the condition
\Delta^{2}\geq c_{\gamma}[\log(n)^{1+\varepsilon}+\sqrt{\frac{pK^{2}}{n}}]
of Proposition
G.3
is large enough and that
c^{\prime}_{\gamma,\epsilon}
such that
n\geq K^{c^{\prime}_{\gamma,\epsilon}}
is also large enough, so that Proposition
G.4
holds when applied to
\hat{p}\left(Y^{(2)}\right)
. Then, we dispose of an partition
\hat{G}^{(2)}
in
\left(I_{2}\right)
computable in polynomial time which is equal to the restriction of
G^{*}
to
I_{2}
with high probability.

For
k\in[K]
, we write
\hat{\mu}_{k}^{oracle}=\frac{1}{|I_{2}\cap G^{*}_{k}|}\sum_{i\in I_{2}\cap G^{*}_{k}}Y_{i}
and
\hat{\mu}_{k}^{(2)}=\frac{1}{|I_{2}\cap\hat{G}_{k}^{(2)}|}\sum_{i\in I_{2}\cap\hat{G}_{k}^{(2)}}Y_{i}
. The next Lemma characterizes a regime on which linear discriminant analysis with the centers
\hat{\mu}_{k}^{oracle}
does perfect classification of
I_{1}
. We refer for example to Section 12.7.1 (page 271) of the textbook
[
34
]
for a proof of this lemma.

Lemma G.5
.

[
34
]

For
i\in I_{1}
, let us define
\hat{k}_{i}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{k\in[K]}\|Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}_{k}^{oracle}\|
, by breaking arbitrarily equality. There exist constants
c_{\gamma}
and
